|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|=|\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]|=|\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]|
.
By definition of search transitions, we have:

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]=\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N_{0}}},\vec{H^{p}}]\to\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{H^{p}}]

for each
p\in\{1,2\}
.

The rest of the proof is to check that

\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]
has a remote focus,
namely that, if its focus is the
\lightning
-focus, the focus is not
entering. This is done by inspecting interaction rules.

•

When the interaction rule

{\dot{N_{0}}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}}
changes the
\checkmark
-focus
to the
\lightning
-focus, this must be the interaction rule in
Figure 9d
,
which means

{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}}
consists of the
\lightning
-focus and its
outgoing operation edge.
The operation edge remains to be a (unique) outgoing edge of
the focus in

\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]
, and hence the
focus is not entering in

\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]
.

•

When the interaction rule

{\dot{N_{0}}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}}
changes the
?
-focus
to the
\lightning
-focus, this must be the interaction rule
in
Figure 18a
,
18b
,
9e
, which means

{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}}=\langle{\dot{N_{0}}}\rangle_{\lightning/?}
.
Because the focus is not entering in

\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]={\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
,
the focus is also not entering in

\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N^{\prime}_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]=\langle\mathcal{C}_{0}[{\dot{N_{0}}},\vec{\chi}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}
.

Proposition 40
.

When the universal abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O},B_{\mathbb{O}})
is
deterministic and refocusing, it satisfies the following,
for any set

\mathbb{C}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\omega}(L,M_{\mathbb{O}}\cup\mathbb{M})

of contexts that is closed under plugging,
any reasonable triple
(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
,
and any pre-template
\vartriangleleft
on focus-free hypernets

\mathcal{H}_{\omega}(L,M_{\mathbb{O}}\backslash\{?,\checkmark,\lightning\})
.

(1)

If
\vartriangleleft
is a
(\mathbb{C},Q,Q^{\prime})
-template and

(\mathbb{C},Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
-robust relative to all rewrite
transitions, then the
\mathbb{C}
-contextual lifting

\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}
is a

(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
-simulation.

(2)

If
\vartriangleleft
is a
(\mathbb{C},Q^{-1},Q^{\prime})
-template
and the converse
\vartriangleleft^{-1}
is

(\mathbb{C},Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
-robust relative to all rewrite
transitions, then the
\mathbb{C}
-contextual lifting

\overline{\vartriangleleft^{-1}}^{\mathbb{C}}
of the converse
is a
(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
-simulation.

Proof F.7

(Proof prelude)
.

Let
({\dot{\mathcal{C}}};\vec{H^{1}};\vec{H^{2}})
be an arbitrary

\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft
,
such that the focus of
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
is remote, and

{\dot{G_{p}}}:={\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]
is
a rooted state for each
p\in\{1,2\}
.
By definition of contextual lifting,

{\dot{G_{1}}}\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{G_{2}}}
, and equivalently,

{\dot{G_{2}}}\mathrel{(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}}{\dot{G_{1}}}
.
Note that

\overline{\vartriangleleft^{-1}}^{\mathbb{C}}=(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}
.

Because
\vartriangleleft
is output-closed,
by

39
(
2
),
the focus is not exiting in
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.
This implies that, if the focus has an incoming edge in

{\dot{G_{1}}}
or
{\dot{G_{2}}}
, the incoming edge must be from

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Because the machine is deterministic and refocusing, rooted states
and transitions
comprise a deterministic abstract rewriting system, in which final
states and stuck states are normal forms. By

20
, from any state, a sequence
of transitions that result in a final state or a stuck state is
unique, if any.

Because
(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
is a reasonable triple,
Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime\prime}
are
reflexive.
By
35
, this implies that

\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}
and
\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}
are reflexive,
and hence

\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}\subseteq{\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}}\circ\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}\circ\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}}}
, and

(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\subseteq{\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}}\circ(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\circ\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}}}
.

Proof F.8

(Proof of the point (
1
))
.

Our goal is to check conditions (A) and (B) of

Appendix F
for the states

{\dot{G_{1}}}\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{G_{2}}}
.

If
{\dot{G_{1}}}
is final, by

39
(
1
),

{\dot{G_{2}}}
is also final.
The condition (A) of
Appendix F
is fulfilled.

If there exists a state
{\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}
such that

{\dot{G_{1}}}\to{\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}
, we show that one of the
conditions (I) and (II) of
Appendix F
is fulfilled,
by case analysis of the focus in
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.

•

When the focus is the
\checkmark
-focus,
or the
?
-focus that is not entering,
by

39
(
3
),
there exists a focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
with a remote focus, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|
and

{\dot{G_{p}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]\to{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]
for each

p\in\{1,2\}
.
We have the following situation, namely the black part of the
diagram below. Showing the magenta part confirms that
the condition (II) of
Appendix F
is fulfilled.

By the determinism,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]={\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}

and

.

When the focus is the
?
-focus that is entering in

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
,

\vartriangleleft
is

Because

({\dot{N_{1}}},{\dot{N_{2}}})
is a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft

up to
(\dot{\simeq}_{Q^{\prime}},\dot{\simeq}_{Q^{\prime}})
, and
states

{\dot{N_{1}}}
and
{\dot{N_{2}}}
are rooted,
there exists a
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}};\vec{H^{\prime 1}};\vec{H^{\prime 2}})

of
\vartriangleleft

with a non-
\lightning
focus, such that

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]
are also rooted,

{\dot{N_{1}}}\mathrel{\dot{\simeq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]
, and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]\mathrel{\dot{\simeq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{N_{2}}}
.
Because
(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
is a reasonable triple,

Q^{\prime}\subseteq Q^{\prime\prime}
, and hence

\mathord{\dot{\simeq}_{Q^{\prime}}}\subseteq\mathord{\dot{\simeq}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Therefore, we have:

{\dot{N_{1}}}\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}{\dot{N_{2}}}.

•

When the focus is the
\lightning
-focus,

{\dot{G_{1}}}\to{\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}
is a rewrite transition,
and by definition of contextual
lifting, the focus is not entering in

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.
Because
\vartriangleleft
is
(\mathbb{C},Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
-robust
relative to all rewrite transitions, and

{\dot{G_{1}}}
and
{\dot{G_{2}}}
are
rooted, we have one of the following two situations
corresponding to (II) and (III) of
Section 8.3
.

–

There exists a stuck state
{\dot{N}}
such that

{\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}\to^{*}{\dot{N}}
.
The condition (I) of
Appendix F
is
satisfied.

–

There exist a quasi-
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{N_{1}}},{\dot{N_{2}}})
of
\vartriangleleft

up to
(\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime}},\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime\prime}})
, whose
focus is not the
\lightning
-focus,
and two numbers
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

(1+k_{1})\mathrel{Q}k_{2}
,

{\dot{G^{\prime}_{1}}}\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}}
, and

{\dot{G_{2}}}\to^{k_{2}}{\dot{N_{2}}}
.
We have the following situation, namely the black part
of the diagram below.
Showing the magenta part confirms that
the condition (II) of
Appendix F
is
fulfilled.

({\dot{N_{1}}},{\dot{N_{2}}})
is a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft

up to
(\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime}},\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime\prime}})
, and

{\dot{N_{1}}}
and

are rooted,

\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}};\vec{H^{\prime 1}};\vec{H^{\prime 2}})

of
\vartriangleleft

with a non-
\lightning

are also rooted,

{\dot{N_{1}}}\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]

.

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]
,
and hence:

{\dot{N_{1}}}\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}{\dot{N_{2}}}.

Proof F.9

(Proof of the point (
2
))
.

It suffices to check the “reverse” of conditions (A) and (B) of

Appendix F
for the states

{\dot{G_{2}}}\mathrel{(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}}{\dot{G_{1}}}
,
namely the following conditions (A’) and (B’).

(A’) If
{\dot{G_{2}}}
is final,
{\dot{G_{1}}}
is also
final.

(B’) If there exists a state
{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}
such that

{\dot{G_{2}}}\to{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}
, one of the following
(I’) and (II’) holds.

(I’)
There exists a stuck state
{\dot{G^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
such that

{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}\to^{*}{\dot{G^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.

(II’)
There exist two states
{\dot{N_{2}}}
and
{\dot{N_{1}}}
,
and numbers
k_{2},k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

{\dot{N_{2}}}\mathrel{(\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}}\circ(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}\circ\mathord{\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}})}{\dot{N_{1}}}
,

(1+k_{2})\mathrel{Q}k_{1}
,

{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}\to^{k_{2}}{\dot{N_{2}}}
, and

{\dot{G_{1}}}\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}}
.

The proof is mostly symmetric to the point (
1
).
Note that there is a one-to-one correspondence between

\mathbb{C}
-specimens of
\vartriangleleft
and

\mathbb{C}
-specimens of
\vartriangleleft^{-1}
;
any
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}_{0}}};\vec{H^{01}};\vec{H^{02}})

of
\vartriangleleft
gives a
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}_{0}}};\vec{H^{02}};\vec{H^{01}})

of
\vartriangleleft^{-1}
.
Because
\vartriangleleft
is output-closed,
its converse
\vartriangleleft^{-1}
is also output-closed.

If
{\dot{G_{2}}}
is final, by

39
(
1
),

{\dot{G_{1}}}
is also final.
The condition (A’) is fulfilled.

If there exists a state
{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}
such that

{\dot{G_{2}}}\to{\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}
, we show that one of the
conditions (I’) and (II’) above is fulfilled,
by case analysis of the focus in
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.

•

When the focus is the
\checkmark
-focus,
or the
?
-focus that is not entering,
by

39
(
3
),
there exists a focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
with a remote focus, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|
and

{\dot{G_{p}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]\to{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{p}}]
for each

p\in\{1,2\}
.
We have the following situation, namely the black part of the
diagram below. Showing the magenta part confirms that
the condition (II’) is fulfilled.

By the determinism,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]={\dot{G^{\prime}_{2}}}
.

(Q,Q^{\prime},Q^{\prime\prime})
is a reasonable triple,
Q
is a preorder
and
1\mathrel{Q}1
.
The context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
satisfies

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|\in\mathbb{C}
, so

({\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}};\vec{H^{2}};\vec{H^{1}})

\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft^{-1}
.
The context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
has a remote focus, and
the states

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]
are both rooted.
Therefore, we have

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]\mathrel{(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]
.

-focus that is entering in

Because

({\dot{N_{2}}},{\dot{N_{1}}})
is a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft^{-1}

up to
(\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime}},\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime\prime}})
, and
states

{\dot{N_{2}}}
and
{\dot{N_{1}}}
are rooted,
there exists a
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}};\vec{H^{\prime 2}};\vec{H^{\prime 1}})

of
\vartriangleleft^{-1}

with a non-
\lightning
focus, such that

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]
are also rooted,

{\dot{N_{2}}}\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]
, and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}{\dot{N_{1}}}
.
This means

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]\mathrel{\overline{\vartriangleleft^{-1}}^{\mathbb{C}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]
,
and hence:

{\dot{N_{2}}}\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 2}}]\mathrel{(\overline{\vartriangleleft}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\vec{H^{\prime 1}}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}{\dot{N_{1}}}.

Appendix G
Sufficient conditions for robustness

A proof of robustness becomes trivial for a specimen with a rewrite
token that gives a non-rooted state.
Thanks to the lemma below, we can show that a state is not rooted, by
checking paths from the token target.

{defi}
[Accessible paths]

•

A path of a hypernet is said to be
accessible
if it
consists of edges whose all sources have type
\star
.

•

An accessible path is called
stable
if the labels
of its edges are included in

\{\mathsf{I}\}\cup\mathbb{O}_{\checkmark}
.

•

An accessible path is called
active
if it starts with
one active operation edge and possibly followed by a stable
path.

Note that box edges and atom edges never appear in an accessible path.

Lemma 41
.

If a state has a rewrite token that is not an incoming edge of
a contraction edge, then the state satisfies the following property:
If there exists an accessible, but not active, path from the token
target, then the state is not rooted.

Proof G.1
.

This is a contraposition of a consequence of

24
and

33
(
2
).

Checking the condition (III) of robustness (see

Section 8.3
) involves finding a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft
up to

(\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime}},\dot{\preceq}_{Q^{\prime\prime}})
,
namely checking the condition (B) of

Section 8.1
(
2
).
The following lemma enables us to use contextual refinement

\preceq^{\mathbb{C}}_{Q^{\prime}}
to yield state refinement

\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}
, via single
\mathbb{C}
-specimens of
a certain pre-template
\vartriangleleft
.

{defi}

A pre-template
\vartriangleleft
is a
trigger
if it satisfies
the following:

(A) For any single
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\chi];H^{1};H^{2})
of

\vartriangleleft
, such that
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
has an
entering search token,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}]\to\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}

for each
p\in\{1,2\}
.

(B) For any hypernets
H^{1}\vartriangleleft H^{2}
, both
H_{1}
and

H_{2}
are one-way.

Lemma 42
.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a set of contexts, and

Q^{\prime}
be a binary relation on
\mathbb{N}
such that,
for any
k_{0},k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
,

(k_{0}+k_{1})\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}(k_{0}+k_{2})
implies

k_{1}\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}k_{2}
.
Let
\vartriangleleft
be a pre-template that is a trigger and
implies contextual refinement
\preceq^{\mathbb{C}}_{Q^{\prime}}
.
For any single
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\chi];H^{1};H^{2})
of

\vartriangleleft
,
if compute transitions are all deterministic, and
one of states
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
is rooted, then
the other state is also rooted, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
.

Proof G.2
.

This is a corollary of
43
.

Lemma 43
.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a set of contexts, and

Q^{\prime}
be a binary relation on
\mathbb{N}
such that,
for any
k_{0},k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
,

(k_{0}+k_{1})\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}(k_{0}+k_{2})
implies

k_{1}\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}k_{2}
.
Let
\vartriangleleft
be a pre-template that is a trigger and
implies contextual refinement
\preceq^{\mathbb{C}}_{Q^{\prime}}
.
For any single
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\chi];H^{1};H^{2})
of

\vartriangleleft
, the following holds.

(1)

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{1}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]

if and only if

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{2}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
.

(2)

If compute transitions are all deterministic, and
one of states
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
is rooted, then the other state
is also rooted, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
.

Proof G.3

(Proof of the point (
1
))
.

Let
(p,q)
be an arbitrary element of a set
\{(1,2),(2,1)\}
.
We prove that, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}]

implies

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{q}]
.
The proof is by case analysis on the number
k
.

•

When
k=0
,
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}]
is initial, and by

39
(
1
),

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{q}]
is also initial.
Note that
\vartriangleleft
is a trigger and hence
output-closed.

•

When
k>0
, by the following internal lemma,

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{q}]

follows from

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}]
.

Lemma 44
.

For any
m\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
,
there exists a focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\chi]
such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|

and the following holds:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k-m}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}],

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{q}].

Proof G.4
.

By induction on
m
.
In the base case, when
m=0
, we can take

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|
as
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
.

In the inductive case, when
m>0
, by induction hypothesis,
there exists a focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[\chi]
such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|

and the following holds:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k-m+1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}],

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{q}].

Because

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|\in\mathbb{C}
,

({\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}};H^{1};H^{2})
is a single

\mathbb{C}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft
, which yields
rooted states.
Because
k-m+1>0
,
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
cannot have a
rewrite token.
The rest of the proof is by case analysis on the token of

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
.

–

When
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
has an entering search
token, because
\vartriangleleft
is a trigger,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{r}]\to\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{r}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}

for each
r\in\{p,q\}
.
Because

\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{r}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}=\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}\rangle_{\lightning/?}[H^{r}]
,
and search transitions are deterministic, we have the
following:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}\rangle_{\lightning/?}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k-m}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}],

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{q}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}\rangle_{\lightning/?}[H^{q}].

We also have

|\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}\rangle_{\lightning/?}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|
.

–

When
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}
has a value token, or a
non-entering search token, because
\vartriangleleft

is output-closed, by

39
(
3
),
there exists a focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}
such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}|=|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}|

and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{r}]\to{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}[H^{r}]

for each
r\in\{p,q\}
.
The transition

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{r}]\to{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}[H^{r}]
,
for each
r\in\{p,q\}
, is a search transition,
and by the determinism of search transitions, we have
the following:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{p}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}[H^{p}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k-m}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{p}],

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{q}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{m-1}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}}[H^{q}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}}[H^{q}].

Proof G.5

(Proof of the point (
2
))
.

If one of states
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]
is rooted, by the
point (
1
),
the other state is also rooted, and moreover, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}

such that

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{r}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{r}]

for each
r\in\{1,2\}
.

Our goal is to prove that, for any
k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
and any final state

{\dot{N_{1}}}
such that

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}}
,
there exist
k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and a final state
{\dot{N_{2}}}
such that

k_{1}\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}k_{2}
and

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]\to^{k_{2}}{\dot{N_{2}}}
.
Assuming
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}}
,
we have the following:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{1}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}},

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{2}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}].

Because
\vartriangleleft
implies contextual refinement

\preceq^{\mathbb{C}}_{Q^{\prime}}
, and

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|\in\mathbb{C}
,
we have state refinement

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{1}]\mathrel{\dot{\preceq}}_{Q^{\prime}}?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{2}]
.
Therefore, there exist
l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and a final state

{\dot{N_{2}}}
such that

(k+k_{1})\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}l_{2}
and

?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{2}]\to^{l_{2}}{\dot{N_{2}}}
.

The assumption that compute transitions are all deterministic
implies that all transitions, including intrinsic ones, are
deterministic. Following from this are
l_{2}\geq k
and the
following:

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{1}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{1}]\to^{k_{1}}{\dot{N_{1}}},

\displaystyle?;|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|[H^{2}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}^{k}{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H^{2}]\to^{l_{2}-k}{\dot{N_{2}}}.

By the assumption on
Q^{\prime}
,
(k+k_{1})\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}l_{2}
implies

k_{1}\mathrel{Q^{\prime}}(l_{2}-k)
.

Appendix H
Local rewrite rules and transfer properties

The sufficiency-of-robustness theorem reduces a proof of an observational
equivalence down to establishing robust templates. As illustrated in

Section 12
, this typically boils down to checking
input-safety of pre-templates, and checking robustness of pre-templates relative
to rewrite transitions.

The key part of checking input-safety or robustness of a pre-template is to
analyse how a rewrite transition involves edges (at any depth) of a state that
are contributed by the pre-template.
In this section, we focus on rewrite transitions that are locally specified by
means of the contraction rules or rewrite rules (e.g. the micro-beta rewrite rules),
and identify some situation where these transitions involve
the edges contributed by a pre-template in a
safe
manner.
These situations can be formalised for arbitrary instances of the universal
abstract machine, including the particular instance

\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}},B_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}})
that is used in
Section 12
to prove the
Parametricity law.

This section proceeds as follows.
Firstly, Appendix
H.1
formalises the safe involvement in
terms of
transfer
properties.
Appendix
H.2
establishes transfer properties for the
particular pre-templates and local rewrite rules used in

Section 12
to prove the Parametricity law.
Finally, Appendix
H.3
demonstrates the use of
the transfer properties, by providing details of
checking input-safety and robustness to prove the Parametricity law.

H.1.
Transfer properties

We refer to the contraction rules which locally specify copy transitions, and
rewrite rules that locally specify rewrite transitions for active operations,
altogether as
\lightning
-rules
.
To analyse how a
\lightning
-rule involves edges contributed by a pre-template,
one would first need to check all possible overlaps between the local rule and
the edges, and then observe how these overlaps are affected by application of
the local rule.
We identify
safe
involvement of the pre-template in the

\lightning
-rule, as the situation where the overlaps get only eliminated or
duplicated without any internal modification.

We will first formalise safe involvement for a single application
of
\lightning
-rules, and then for a pair of applications of

\lightning
-rules. The latter can capture safe involvement of edges contributed
by a pre-template, which can be exploited to check input-safety and
robustness of pre-templates.

Notation H.0
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given a sequence
\vec{x}=x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}
of length
m
and a function

f\colon\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}\to\{1,\ldots,m\}
, a sequence

f(\vec{x})=x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}}
of
length
m^{\prime}
is given by
x^{\prime}_{j}=x_{f(j)}
for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}
.

{defi}

[Transfer of hypernets]

Let
\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
be two sets of focus-free contexts, and

\mathbb{H}
be a set of focus-free hypernets.
A
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
of a universal
abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O},B_{\mathbb{O}})

transfers
\mathbb{H}
from
\mathbb{C}
to
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
if,
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
any focussed context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{m}]
such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}|\in\mathbb{C}
,
and any
m
focus-free hypernets
G_{i}\in\mathbb{H}

(
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
) such that

{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}]
,
there exist some
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
some focussed context
{\dot{\mathcal{C^{\prime}}}}[\chi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\chi^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}}]
,
and some function
f\colon\{1,\ldots,m^{\prime}\}\to\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
and the following holds.

•

|{\dot{\mathcal{C^{\prime}}}}|\in\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
.

•

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C^{\prime}}}}[f(G_{1},\ldots,G_{m})]
.

•

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[H_{1},\ldots,H_{m}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C^{\prime}}}}[f(H_{1},\ldots,H_{m})]
is a
\lightning
-rule,
for any
m
focus-free hypernets
H_{i}\in\mathbb{H}

(
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
).

This transfer property enjoys monotonicity in the following sense:
if a
\lightning
-rule transfers
\mathbb{H}
from
\mathbb{C}
to

\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
, and
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathbb{C^{\prime\prime}}
, then
the
\lightning
-rule transfers
\mathbb{H}
from
\mathbb{C}
to

\mathbb{C^{\prime\prime}}
as well.
If a
\lightning
-rule transfers
\mathbb{H}
from
\mathbb{C}
to the same

\mathbb{C}
, we say the
\lightning
-rule

preserves
\mathbb{H}
in
\mathbb{C}
.

Given an operation set
\mathbb{O}
, we will be particularly interested in the
following sets of hypernets and contexts for
\mathbb{O}
, some of which have already
been introduced elsewhere:
the set
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}
of all focus-free hypernets,
the set
\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
of contraction trees,
the set
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
of all focus-free contexts,
the set
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
of all binding-free contexts,
the set
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
of all
deep
contexts, i.e. focus-free
contexts whose holes are all deep.

{exa}

[Transfer/preservation of hypernets in contexts]

•

When a
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
preserves

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
, any deep edge of

{\dot{N}}
also appears as a deep edge in
{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
, and it
also retains its neighbours.
This is trivially the case if the
\lightning
-rule involves no box
edges (and hence deep edges) at all. It is also the case if the

\lightning
-rule only eliminates or duplicates box edges without
modifying deep edges. The contraction rules are an example of
duplicating boxes.

•

Preservation of deep edges can be restricted to binding-free positions,
which are specified by binding-free contexts.
When a
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
preserves

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
,
any deep edge of
{\dot{N}}
in a binding-free position also appears
as a deep edge in a binding-free position in
{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
.

•

When a
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
transfers

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}
from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
, any
deep edge of
{\dot{N}}
also appears as an edge in
{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
,
retaining its neighbours, but not necessarily as a deep edge.
This is preservation of deep edges in a weak sense.
It is the case when a
\lightning
-rule replaces a box edge with its
contents, turning some deep edges into shallow edges without modifying
their connection. The micro-beta rewrite rules are an example of this
situation.

•

When a
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
preserves

\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
, any contraction tree in

{\dot{N}}
also appears in
{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
. The contraction rules
are designed to satisfy this preservation property.
∎

{defi}

[Transfer of (rooted) specimens]

Let
\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
be two sets of focus-free contexts, and

\vartriangleleft
be a pre-template.

•

A
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
of a universal
abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O},B_{\mathbb{O}})

transfers specimens of
\vartriangleleft
from
\mathbb{C}
to

\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
if,
for any
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of the form

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

such that
{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
,
there exist some focussed context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}
and
two sequences
\vec{G^{\prime\prime\prime}}
and
\vec{H^{\prime\prime\prime}}
of focus-free hypernets, and
the following holds.

–

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime\prime}}]
.

–

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime\prime}}]

is a
\lightning
-rule.

–

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime\prime}})

is a
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft
.

•

The
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
is said to
transfer
rooted
specimens of
\vartriangleleft
from
\mathbb{C}

to
\mathbb{C^{\prime}}
if, in the above definition, the
\mathbb{C}
-specimen

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

is restricted to yield two rooted states

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{G^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]]
and

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{H^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]]
.

If a
\lightning
-rule transfers specimens of
\vartriangleleft
from

\mathbb{C}
to the same
\mathbb{C}
, we say the
\lightning
-rule

preserves specimens of
\vartriangleleft
in
\mathbb{C}
.

We can prove that certain transfer properties of hypernets imply the
corresponding transfer properties of specimens, as stated in

45
below.
These are primarily transfer of deep edges, and preservation of contraction
trees.

45
below will simplify some part of establishing
input-safety and robustness of a pre-template, because it
enables us to analyse a single application of a

\lightning
-rule on a state, instead of a pair of applications of a

\lightning
-rule on two states induced by a specimen of the pre-template.

{defi}

[Root-focussed
\lightning
-rules]
A
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
is said to be

root-focussed
if it satisfies the following.

•

{\dot{N}}
has only one input.

•

{\dot{N}}=\lightning;|{\dot{N}}|
holds, i.e. the sole input
of
{\dot{N}}
coincides with the source of the token.

•

Every output of
{\dot{N}}
is reachable from the sole input of

{\dot{N}}
.

Proposition 45
.

For any
\lightning
-rule
{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
of a universal
abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O},B_{\mathbb{O}})
, the following holds.

(1)

If it transfers
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
,
it transfers specimens of any pre-template
\vartriangleleft

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
.

(2)

If it preserves
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
,
it preserves specimens of any pre-template

\vartriangleleft

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

(3)

If it is root-focussed and transfers
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
,
it transfers rooted specimens of any output-closed pre-template

\vartriangleleft

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

(4)

If it preserves
\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
,
it preserves specimens of any pre-template

\mathord{\vartriangleleft}\subseteq\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}\times\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
,
which is on contraction trees,
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
.

Proof H.1

(Proof of the point (
1
))
.

We take an arbitrary
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
-specimen of the form

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

of the pre-template
\vartriangleleft
, such that

{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
.
Because the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]]
of the
specimen must be focussed, the token in the context is shallow. This means that
the hole labelled with
\chi
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},\chi]
must be shallow.
On the other hand, the specimen satisfies

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
and hence

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
.
As a consequence, we have

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
.
Now, by the assumption, there exist some focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}
and some function
f
, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
and

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]
hold, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})]

is also a
\lightning
-rule.
We obtain a triple

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}];\vec{G^{\prime}},f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}});\vec{H^{\prime}},f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}))
.
It satisfies

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}]|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
,
and is a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
-specimen of the pre-template
\vartriangleleft
.

Proof H.2

(Proof of the point (
2
))
.

We take an arbitrary

(\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}})
-specimen of the form

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

of the pre-template
\vartriangleleft
, such that

{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
.

We first check that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

follows from

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
, as follows.

•

Because the context
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]

must be focussed, the token in the context is shallow. This means that
the hole labelled with
\chi
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},\chi]
must be shallow.
This, combined with

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]|=\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
implies
|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
.

•

If the context
|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|
contains a path that makes
it not binding-free, the path is also a path in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]
and makes the
context not binding-free.
Therefore, because

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]|=\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
holds,
the context
|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|
is without any path that
makes the context not binding-free.
This means

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

By the assumption, there exist some focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}
and some function
f
, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
and

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]
hold, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})]

is also a
\lightning
-rule.
We obtain a triple

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}];\vec{G^{\prime}},f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}});\vec{H^{\prime}},f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}))
.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to prove that this triple is a

(\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}})
-specimen of the
pre-template
\vartriangleleft
,
which boils down to showing

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}]|=\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

We firstly prove
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
.
Because
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
holds,
the holes labelled with
\vec{\chi^{\prime}}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}
must be all deep. This, together with

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
implies
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
.

We then prove
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
by contradiction,
which will conclude the whole proof.
Assume that the context is not binding-free. It has a path
P
from a source of
an edge
e
that is either a contraction edge, an atom edge, a box edge or a
hole edge, to a source of an edge
e^{\prime}
that is a hole edge.
Thanks to
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
and

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
the hole edge
e^{\prime}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

must be deep.
We will infer a contradiction by case analysis on the hole edge
e^{\prime}
.
There are two cases.

•

One case is when the edge
e^{\prime}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

comes from the context
\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
In this case, the edge
e^{\prime}
is one of the deep hole edges
labelled with
\vec{\chi^{\prime}}
.
This means that the path
P
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

must consist of deep edges only, and these deep edges, together
with the edge
e^{\prime}
, must be contained in a box of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
Therefore the path
P
is also a path in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}
, and it makes the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]
not
binding-free. This contradicts

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

•

The other case is when the edge
e^{\prime}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

comes from the context
|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
.
In this case, the edge
e^{\prime}
is a hole edge of the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
, and
hence a deep edge.
This means that the path
P
is also a path in the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
, consisting of deep edges only.
The path
P
therefore makes the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
not binding-free, which contradicts

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

Proof H.3

(Proof of the point (
3
))
.

We take an arbitrary

(\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}})
-specimen of the form

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

of the pre-template
\vartriangleleft
, such that

{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
holds,
and two states

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{G^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]]
and

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{H^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]]
are both rooted.

We can first check

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
,
in the same way as the proof of the point (
2
).
By the assumption, there exist some focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}
and some function
f
, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
and

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]
hold, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})]

is also a
\lightning
-rule.
We obtain a triple

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}];\vec{G^{\prime}},f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}});\vec{H^{\prime}},f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}))
.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to prove that this triple is a

\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
-specimen of the pre-template
\vartriangleleft
,
which boils down to showing

|\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}]|=\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.
We prove this by contradiction, as follows.

Assume that the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

is not binding-free. It has a path
P
from a source of
an edge
e
that is either a contraction edge, an atom edge, a box edge or a
hole edge, to a source of an edge
e^{\prime}
that is a hole edge.
We will infer a contradiction by case analysis on the edge
e^{\prime}
.
There are two cases.

•

One case is when the edge
e^{\prime}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

comes from the context
\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
In this case, the edge
e^{\prime}
is one of the hole edges labelled with

\vec{\chi^{\prime}}
.
Because of
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
the hole edge
e^{\prime}
must be deep.
This means that the path
P
must consist of deep edges contained in a
box of the context
\mathcal{C}_{1}
. The path is therefore a path in
the context
\mathcal{C}_{1}
, and also in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]
.
This means

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|]\notin\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
, which is a contradiction.

•

The other case is when the edge
e^{\prime}
of the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

comes from the context
|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
.
In this case, we will infer a contradiction by further case analysis on
the edge
e
and the path
P
. There are three (sub-)cases.

–

The first case is when the edge
e
comes from the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
and
P
is a path in the context.
In this case, the path
P
makes the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
not binding-free, which contradicts

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

–

The second case is when the edge
e
comes from the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
and
P
does not give a single path
in the context.
In this case, the edges
e
and
e^{\prime}
both come from the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
, but
P
is a valid path only in
the whole context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]
.
This means that, in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},\chi]
, a source of the hole edge
labelled with
\chi
is reachable from a target of the same hole
edge.

Because the
\lightning
-rule

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]

is root-focussed, the focussed hypernet

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
has only one input, the
input coincides with the source of the token, and
every output of the hypernet is reachable from the sole input.
Moreover, because of

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
the same holds for the focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}
too, namely:
the context has only one input, the input coincides with the
source of the token, and every output of the
context is reachable from the sole input.

As a consequence, in the focussed context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]
,
the token source is reachable from itself, via a cyclic path
that contains some edges coming from the context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}
including the token edge.
This path is not an operation path. Therefore, by

26
(
3
),
at least one of the states

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{G^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]]
and

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{H^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]]

is not rooted. This is a contradiction.

–

The last case is when the edge
e
comes from the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
Recall that the edge
e^{\prime}
comes from the context

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|
.
In this case, the path
P
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]

has a prefix that gives a path
P^{\prime}
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},\chi]
, from the same source of the
edge
e
as the path
P
, to a source of the hole
edge labelled with
\chi
.
Because the path
P^{\prime}
is given as a part of the path
P
in the
context
\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|]
,
the path
P^{\prime}
in the context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},\chi]
does not itself contain the
hole edge labelled with
\chi
.

Because the
\lightning
-rule

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]

is root-focussed, the focussed hypernet

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
has only one input, and the
input coincides with the source of the token.
Moreover, because of

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
,
the same holds for the focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}
too, namely:
the context has only one input, and the input coincides with the
source of the token.

As a consequence, the path
P^{\prime}
in turn gives a path in the
focussed context

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}]
,
from the same source of the edge
e
as the path
P
,
to the source of the token. The first edge
e
of this path is
not an operation edge, and therefore the path is not an operation
path. By

26
(
3
),
at least one of the states

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{G^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]]
and

\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{H^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]]

is not rooted. This is a contradiction.

Proof H.4

(Proof of the point (
4
))
.

We take an arbitrary
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
-specimen of the form

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{\chi^{\prime\prime}}]];\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}};\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})

of the pre-template

\mathord{\vartriangleleft}\subseteq\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}\times\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
,
such that
{\dot{N}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{G^{\prime\prime}}]
.
All the hypernets in
\vec{G^{\prime}},\vec{G^{\prime\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime}},\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}
are elements of

\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
, i.e. contraction trees. It trivially holds that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
.
Therefore, by the assumption, there exist some focussed context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}
and some function
f
, such that

|{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}|\in\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
and

{\dot{N^{\prime}}}={\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}})]
hold, and moreover,

{\dot{\mathcal{C}_{2}}}[\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}]\mapsto{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}[f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}})]

is also a
\lightning
-rule.
We obtain a triple

(\mathcal{C}_{1}[\vec{\chi^{\prime}},{\dot{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}}}];\vec{G^{\prime}},f(\vec{G^{\prime\prime}});\vec{H^{\prime}},f(\vec{H^{\prime\prime}}))
,
and this is a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}}
-specimen of the pre-template

\vartriangleleft
.

H.2.
Transfer properties for the Parametricity law

We can now establish transfer properties of deep edges and contraction trees for
the particular machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}},B_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}})
which is used to prove the
Parametricity law.

Proposition 46
.

The universal abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}},B_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}})
satisfies the
following.

(1)

The contraction rules and all rewrite rules
transfer
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
.

(2)

The contraction rules, and all rewrite rules except for the micro-beta rewrite
rules,
preserve
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{ex}}

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

(3)

The micro-beta rewrite rules
transfer
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{ex}}

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

(4)

The contraction rules and all rewrite rules
preserve
\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}

in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
.

Proof H.5

(Sketch of the proof)
.

We can prove the four points by analysing each
\lightning
-rule, i.e. a
contraction rule or a local rewrite rule for an active operation, of the
universal abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}},B_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}})
.

Firstly, the only way in which a contraction rule involves deep edges is to
have them inside the hypernet to be duplicated (
H
in

Figure 17
).
The deep edges and their connection are all preserved, and replacing these
edges with arbitrary deep edges still enables the contraction rule. The
point (
1
) therefore holds.
Additionally, any path to a source of a
deep edge must consist of deep edges only, and if such a path appears in the
result
{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
of a contraction rule

{\dot{N}}\mapsto{\dot{N^{\prime}}}
, the path necessarily appears in the
original hypernet
{\dot{N}}
too. Therefore, if the contraction rule
moves deep edges out of binding-free positions, these edges must not be at
binding-free positions beforehand. This is a contradiction, and
the point (
2
) holds.
As for contraction trees, whenever a contraction rule involves a contraction
tree, the tree is either deep and gets duplicated, or shallow and left
unmodified. Replacing the contraction tree with another contraction tree still
enables the contraction rule that duplicates the same hypernet.
The point (
4
) therefore holds.

Secondly, we analyse the micro-beta rewrite rules.
Whenever deep edges are involved in a micro-beta rewrite rule, they must be inside
the box edge that gets opened (i.e.
G
in
Figure 10b
).
These deep edges may be turned into shallow edges, but their connection is
unchanged. The difference of deep edges does not affect application of the
rule, and hence the point (
1
) holds.
If these deep edges are at binding-free positions, they remain at binding-free
positions after applying the micro-beta rewrite rule, for a similar reason as the
contraction rules. The point (
3
) therefore holds.
As for contraction trees,
the only way in which contraction trees get involved in a micro-beta rewrite rule is
for them to be deep. The point (
4
) reduces to the
point (
1
) for micro-beta rewrite rules.

The rest of the local rewrite rules involve no deep edges at all, and therefore
points (
1
) and (
2
)
trivially hold. These rules either involve no contraction trees, or involve
shallow contraction trees without any modification. The difference of
contraction trees does not affect application of the rules. The
point (
4
) therefore holds.

Corollary 47
.

In the universal abstract machine
\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}},B_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}})
, the contraction
rules and all rewrite rules satisfies the following.

(1)

The rules
transfer specimens of any pre-template
\vartriangleleft

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
.

(2)

The rules
transfer rooted specimens of any output-closed pre-template

\vartriangleleft

from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

(3)

The rules
preserve specimens of any pre-template

\mathord{\vartriangleleft}\subseteq\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}\times\mathbb{H}_{\otimes}
,
which is on contraction trees,
in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
.

Proof H.6
.

This is a consequence of
46
and

45
, noting that the micro-beta rewrite rules are root-focussed
and preserving in
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
implies
transferring from
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{dp}}}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}

to
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.

Triggers and their implied contextual

In this section we give some details of proving
input-safety and robustness of the pre-templates for the Parametricity law,
as indicated in
Table 3
.
The proofs exploit the transfer properties established in

47
.

Figure 24
lists triggers that we use to prove
input-safety and robustness of some of the pre-templates
7
7

7

The numbering of triggers is according to the one used
in
[
Mur20
, Section 4.5.5]
. Some of triggers in
loc. cit.
are
for observational equivalences that we do not consider in this paper, and hence
not presented here.
.

shows contextual
refinements/equivalences implied by these triggers
(in the “implication” column), given that some

.

shows which pre-template requires

(in the “used for” column).
Note that the converse of any trigger is again a trigger.

Recall that there is a choice of contraction trees upon applying a
contraction rule and some of the local rewrite rules.
The minimum choice is to collect only contraction edges whose target
is reachable from the token target.
The maximum choice is to take the contraction tree(s) so that no
contraction or weakening edge is incoming to the unique hole edge in a
context.

H.3.1.
Pre-templates on contraction trees

First we check input-safety and robustness of

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}\mathrm{Assoc}}
,

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}\mathrm{Comm}}
and

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}\mathrm{Idem}}
,
which are all on contraction trees.

Input-safety of

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}\mathrm{Assoc}}
and

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}\mathrm{Comm}}
can be checked as follows.
Given a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}}};\vec{H^{1}};\vec{H^{2}})
with an entering search
token,
because any input of a contraction tree is a source of a contraction
edge, we have:

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]\rangle_{\lightning/?},\quad{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}.

It can be observed that
a rewrite transition is possible in

\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}

if and only if
a rewrite transition is possible in

\langle{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{2}}]\rangle_{\lightning/?}
.
When a rewrite transition is possible in both states, we can use

47
(
3
), by considering
a maximal possible contraction rule.
The results of the rewrite transition can be given by a new
quasi-
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen up to
(=,=)
(here
=
denotes
equality on states).
When no rewrite transition is possible, both of the states are not
final but stuck.

Robustness of the three pre-templates and their converse can also be
proved using
47
(
3
),
by considering a maximal possible local (contraction or rewrite) rule
in each case.

H.3.2.
Input-safety of pre-templates not on
contraction trees

As mentioned in
Section 12.2
,
pre-templates that relate hypernets with no input of type
\star
are
trivially input-safety for any parameter
(\mathbb{C},Q,Q^{\prime})
.
This leaves us pre-templates
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}
,

\vartriangleleft^{\mathop{\overset{\rightarrow}{@}}}
,
\vartriangleleft^{\mathtt{ref}}

and
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrm{Param}}

to check.

As for
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}
,
note that the pre-template
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}
relates
hypernets with at least one input.
Any
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen of
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}

with an entering search token can be turned into the form

(\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}\chi),\vec{\chi}];H^{1},\vec{H^{1}};H^{2},\vec{H^{2}})

where
j
is a positive number.
The proof is by case analysis on the number
j
.

•

When
j=1
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\lightning;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{H^{1}}].

We can take

(\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{\chi}];\vec{H^{1}};\vec{H^{2}})

as a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen, and
the token in
\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{\chi}]
is not
entering.

•

When
j>1
, the token target must be a source of a
contraction edge. There exist
a focus-free context
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[\chi^{\prime}]
,
two focus-free hypernets

H^{\prime 1}\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}H^{\prime 2}
and
a focus-free hypernet
G
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\lightning;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}[H^{\prime 1}]),\vec{H^{1}}],

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{H^{2}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\lightning;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{H^{2}}]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}G),\vec{H^{2}}],

and

\mathcal{C^{\prime}}[H^{\prime 2}]\mathrel{\dot{\simeq}}_{=_{\mathbb{N}}}G

given by the trigger
\vartriangleleft^{1}

via
42
.
The results of these sequences give a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen
up to
(\mathord{=},\mathord{\dot{\simeq}_{=_{\mathbb{N}}}})
.

A proof of input-safety of the operational pre-templates

\vartriangleleft^{\mathop{\overset{\rightarrow}{@}}}
and
\vartriangleleft^{\mathtt{ref}}
is a
simpler version of that of
\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}
,
because the operational pre-templates relate hypernets with only one
input.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be either
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
or

\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
.
Any
\mathbb{C}
-specimen of an operational pre-template
with an entering search token can be turned into the form

(\mathcal{C}[(?;\chi),\vec{\chi}];H^{1},\vec{H^{1}};H^{2},\vec{H^{2}})
;
note that the parameter
j
that we had for

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrel{\raisebox{0.2pt}{\scalebox{.6}{\boldmath$\otimes$}}}}
is redundant in
?;\chi
.
We have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\lightning;H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{C}[(?;H^{2}),\vec{H^{1}}].

We can take

(\mathcal{C}[(?;H^{2}),\vec{\chi}];\vec{H^{1}};\vec{H^{2}})

as a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen, and
the token in
\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{\chi}]
is not
entering.
This data gives a
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
-specimen
when
\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
,
which follows from the closedness of
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}{\text{-}\mathrm{bf}}}
with
respect to plugging (
34
).
Note that
?;H^{1}
can be seen as a context with no holes, which is
trivially binding-free.

Finally, we look at the parametricity pre-template

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrm{Param}}
.
Any
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen of this pre-template,
with an entering search token, can be turned into the form

(\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}\chi),\vec{\chi}];

H^{1},\vec{H^{1}};H^{2},\vec{H^{2}})

where
j
is a positive number.
The token target is a source of an edge labelled with

\lambda\in\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\checkmark}
, so we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\checkmark;_{j}H^{1}),\vec{H^{1}}],

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[(?;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{H^{2}}]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\bullet\,$}}}{{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$\rightarrow$}}}}\mathcal{C}[(\checkmark;_{j}H^{2}),\vec{H^{2}}].

The results of these sequences give a
quasi-
\mathbb{C}_{\mathbb{O}^{\mathrm{ex}}}
-specimen
up to
(\mathord{=},\mathord{=})
.

H.3.3.
Robustness of pre-templates not on
contraction trees: a principle

Robustness can be checked by inspecting rewrite transition

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]\to{\dot{N^{\prime}}}

from the state given by a specimen

({\dot{\mathcal{C}}};\vec{H^{1}};\vec{H^{2}})
of a pre-template,
where the token of
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
is not entering.
We in particular consider the minimum local (contraction or rewrite)
rule
{\dot{G}}\mapsto{\dot{G^{\prime}}}
applied in this transition.
This means that, in the hypernet
{\dot{G}}
, every vertex is
reachable from the token target.

The inspection boils down to analyse how the minimum local rule
involves edges that come from the hypernets
\vec{H^{1}}
.
If all the involvement is deep, i.e. only deep edges from
\vec{H^{1}}

are involved in the local rule, these deep edges must come via deep
holes in the context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
. We can use

47
(
1
).

If the minimum local rule involves shallow edges that are from

\vec{H^{1}}
, endpoints of these edges are reachable from the token
target. This means that, in the context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
, some
holes are shallow and their sources are reachable from the token
target.
Moreover, given that the token is not entering in

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
, the context has a path from the token target
to a source of a hole edge.

(a)
A shallow overlap where
C
is a contraction tree and
B_{i}
are box edges

(b)
A focussed context where
C
is a contraction tree

For example, in checking robustness of

\vartriangleleft^{\mathrm{BPerm}}
with respect to copy transitions,
one situation of shallow overlaps is when
{\dot{G}}
is in the
form in
Figure 25a
, and some of the box edges

B_{i}
are from
\vec{H^{1}}
.
Taking the minimum contraction rule means that
C
in the graph is a
contraction tree that gives a path from the token target.
This path
C
followed by the operation edge
\phi
corresponds to
paths from the token target to hole sources in the context

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
.

So, if the minimum local rule involves shallow edges that
are from
\vec{H^{1}}
, the context
{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}
necessarily
has a path
P
from the token target to a hole source.
The path becomes a path in the state

{\dot{\mathcal{C}}}[\vec{H^{1}}]
, from the token target to a source
of an edge
e
that is from
\vec{H^{1}}
.
The edge
e
is necessarily shallow, and also involved in the
application of the minimum local rule, because of the connectivity of

{\dot{G}}
.
Moreover, a source of the edge
e
is an input, in the relevant
hypernet of
\vec{H^{1}}
.
By inspecting minimum local rules, we can enumerate possible
labelling of the path
P
and the edge
e
, as summarised in

Table 6
.
